--- Model Response ---
The two black nodes in the graph are labeled H and E. I will perform a breadth-first search (BFS) to determine the shortest path between these two nodes.

### Step-by-step reasoning:

1. **Level 1: Start from node H**
   - H is directly connected to the following neighbors: G, I, and P.

2. **Level 2: Neighbors of H**
   - From G: G is connected to F, K, and X. None of these nodes are E.
   - From I: I is connected to C and P. None of these nodes are E.
   - From P: P is connected to I, B, and E.

3. **Level 3: Neighbors of P (we skip G and I since P leads us directly to E)**
   - From P: P is connected to E.

**Identified Path**: H → P → E
This path has a length of 2 edges.

### Verifying Shortest Path:

- **Check other possible paths:**
  - H → G → F → K → X (4 edges, longer)
  - H → G → K → X (3 edges, longer)
  - H → I → C → B → O → Y (5 edges, longer)
  - H → I → C → B → P → E (4 edges, longer)
  - H → P → B → O → Y (4 edges, longer)

Given these explorations, all valid paths originating from H and reaching E, the one identified above is the shortest.

### Final Answer:
Length: 2, Path: [H, P, E]

   - Nodes B, N, O, C, I, F, and X appear in multiple paths.

--- Model Response ---
### Step-by-Step Reasoning:

1. **Identify Black Nodes:**
   The two black nodes in the graph are labeled `E` and `J`.

2. **Trace Paths:**
   - **Paths from `E`:**
     1. `E` -> `B` -> `C` -> `O` -> `P` -> `J`
     2. `E` -> `B` -> `C` -> `N` -> `Y` -> `D` -> `P` -> `J`
     3. `E` -> `B` -> `H` -> `I` -> `P` -> `J`
     4. `E` -> `F` -> `X` -> `G` -> `H` -> `I` -> `P` -> `J`

3. **Analyze and Block Paths:**
   - The paths from `E` to `J` converge at several key nodes: `B`, `H`, and `P`.
   - Removing `B`, `H`, or `P` individually does not disconnect `E` and `J` because alternative paths still exist.
   - Removing `P` alone will not suffice, keeping the other paths intact.

4. **Determine Minimum Cut Set:**
   - Paths are reduced as follows:
     - By removing `B`: `E` -> `F` -> `X` -> `G` -> `H` -> `I` -> `P` -> `J` remains.
     - By removing `H`: `E` -> `B` -> `C` -> `O` -> `P` -> `J` and `E` -> `B` -> `C` -> `N` -> `Y` -> `D` -> `P` -> `J` remain.
     - By simultaneously removing `B` and `H`: no valid path remains.

Based on the above analysis, the minimum number of nodes required to be removed to ensure no path exists between nodes `E` and `J` is 2.

### Final Answer:
**Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [B, H]**

Task: task3_min_edges_removal
Model: mistral-small-3.1-24b

--- Model Response ---
### Step-by-Step Reasoning:

The target nodes are G and I. To disconnect G and I, we need to remove edges such that no path exists between them. Let's identify all possible paths between G and I and determine the minimum number of edges to remove to break all these paths.

1. **Identify Direct Connections:**
   - G is directly connected to F, E, and H.
   - I is directly connected to H, J, P, and C.

2. **Explore Paths:**
   - Path 1: G → E → H → I
   - Path 2: G → F → E → H → I
   - Path 3: G → H → I
   - Path 4: G → E → H → C → I
   - Path 5: G → E → H → C → N → O → P → I
   - Path 6: G → E → M → L → J → P → I

3. **Determine Critical Edges:**
   - To disconnect G and I, we need to remove edges that are common in all paths.
   - Removing (E, H) will break Paths 1, 2, 3, and 4.
   - Removing (H, C) will break Path 4.
   - Removing (M, L) or (L, J) or (J, P) will break Path 6.
   - However, removing (H, C) and (E, H) will also break remaining paths effectively.

4. **Minimal Cut Set:**
   - By removing (E, H) and (H, C), we ensure that all possible paths between G and I are broken.

### Final Answer:
Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(E, H), (H, C)]
--- Ground Truth ---
{'length': 2, 'cut_set': [['F', 'E'], ['G', 'H']]}

   - To disconnect Path 3: Remove (G, L) or (J, I).

4. **Minimal Cut Set:**
   - Removing (E, H) directly disconnects the direct path.
   - To ensure no alternative paths exist, we must remove additional critical edges. The most efficient way is to cut edges that appear in multiple paths. For example:
     - Remove (E, H) to cut the direct path.
     - Remove (J, I) to cut Path 2 and Path 3.

By removing these two edges, we ensure that there are no remaining paths between E and H.

### Final Answer:

```plaintext

```
